    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

-f \\\"$BASE_RESULT\\\" ]; then\\n    echo \\\"=== Evaluating base model (no adapter) ===\\\"\\n    mkdir -p \\\"${OUTPUT_BASE}/base_model\\\"\\n    python3 \\\"${SCRIPTS_DIR}/exp006_evaluate.py\\\" \\\\\\n        --model-name \\\"$MODEL\\\" \\\\\\n        --output \\\"$BASE_RESULT\\\" \\\\\\n        --cache-dir \\\"$CACHE_DIR\\\"\\nfi\\n\\n# Count runs\\ntotal_runs=0\\nfor s in $STRATEGIES; do\\n    for seed in $SEEDS; do\\n        if [ \\\"$s\\\" = \\\"graduated\\\" ]; then\\n            for _ in $ALPHAS; do total_runs=$((total_runs + 1)); done\\n        else\\n            total_runs=$((total_runs + 1))\\n        fi\\n    done\\ndone\\n\\n# ~25 min per run (15 min train + 10 min eval on 4090 with 1.5B LoRA)\\nest_hours=$(echo \\\"scale=1; $total_runs * 25 / 60\\\" | bc)\\necho \\\"=== exp006 Graduated Filtering Evaluation ===\\\"\\necho \\\"Strategies: $STRATEGIES\\\"\\necho \\\"Seeds:      $SEEDS\\\"\\necho \\\"Alphas:     $ALPHAS (graduated only)\\\"\\necho \\\"Total runs: $total_runs\\\"\\necho \\\"Est. 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. ```text
OK, compute_coverage_matrix failed due to a technical issue. Let me focus on the key findings.

Let me now synthesize my review findings:

## Critical findings:

### 1. Registry vs Experiment Plan Data Inconsistency
The registry has null values for:
- exp_003 config4_t12: 0v1/2v3/3v4/4v5 AUC and top3_features all null
- exp_007 qwen_cont 0v1: ci_lower, ci_upper, perm_p all null

But experiment_plan shows filled values for both. 

The worker likely updated experiment_plan.yaml but didn't sync those changes back to registry.yaml. There's also a mismatch in the config counts—registry shows 7 configs while experiment plan shows 8, and since paper_framing.md references 8 conditions, the registry is out of sync with what the paper actually describes. The data itself exists, so it's not critical, but it's a warning sign that the source of truth needs updating.

### 2. exp_006 Runtime Estimate
The script estimates around 25 minutes per run, but looking at actual performance, training is progressing at 986 out of 2814 steps in 12 minutes, which suggests the estimate might be off.

Looking at the actual numbers, training will take closer to 36 minutes, evaluation adds another 10 minutes, so each run is more like 46 minutes total. That means 50 runs would take roughly 38 hours instead of the 18 hours the ROADMAP claims. This pushes completion to sometime between 05-15 17:00 and 20:00 UTC, with writing finishing by 05-16 at the earliest. Still within the deadline, but the timeline is optimistic by about a day.

### 3. GPU Compliance
The GPU is running at 84% utilization with 15GB of 24GB in use on a single GPU machine, so there's no compliance issue.

### 4. exp_006 Registry Status
Registry shows the experiment as ALLOCATED, but the process is actively running with PID 46129.

### 5. Experiment Plan Bookkeeping
Both exp-006 and exp-011 have null registry_ids in the experiment plan despite existing in the registry under different names (exp_006_graduated_filtering and exp_011_llm_zeroshot respectively).

### 6. Coverage Matrix Tool Error
The coverage matrix computation failed because some experiments store claims as lists rather than single values, which breaks the tool's assumptions. This is a tool bug rather than a project issue.

Now I'm ready to compile everything into a review for the director.
```

computed via external scorer model on tokenized text\\\"\\n      - \\\"§3.3 Ordinal Binary Decomposition Classifier: OBD-LightGBM — K binary classifiers for K+1 depth levels; ordinal structure enforced by cumulative probability decomposition; advantage over multiclass (exp-010: lower MAE)\\\"\\n      - \\\"§3.4 K* Operationalization: K*_empirical = max k s.t. ```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We presented the first systematic measurement of the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.
5	Across 8 configurations spanning 3 model families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct), 2 generation modes, and 4 decoding strategies, we find $\Kempirical = 2$ uniformly: classifiers can reliably distinguish adjacent generations up to depth~2, but beyond this boundary adjacent depths are operationally indistinguishable ($\auc$ below $\theta = 0.60$).
6	The 0v1 boundary is near-perfectly separable ($\auc > 0.99$), followed by a sharp drop of approximately 30 percentage points at 1v2 ($\auc$: 0.6112--0.6955).
7	Cross-model classifiers retain 80--92\% of within-model accuracy among continuation-mode models, with 0v1 transfer $\auc$ exceeding 0.96 universally---indicating that depth signals are largely text-intrinsic.
8	Feature degradation orderings are highly consistent across model families (Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$), with surprisal-variance features dominating uniformly.
9	The boundary is invariant to decoding strategy, though feature importance rankings diverge between low- and high-entropy sampling ($\rho = 0.914$ for greedy vs.\ $T{=}0.7$; $\rho = 0.53$ for greedy vs.\ $T{=}1.2$).
10	Frontier LLMs achieve approximately 88\% binary human-vs.-synthetic accuracy but only 33\% on 5-class depth estimation, below the OBD-LightGBM baseline of 38.6\%, confirming that depth estimation is a distinct and harder task than binary detection.
11	
12	% [TODO: graduated filtering conclusion pending exp-006 results.
13	% Will summarize: whether coarse 3-class ordinal signal (human / shallow / deep)
14	% improves downstream task performance over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.]
15	
16	Our results are bounded by the scale of models tested ($\leq$1.5B parameters), the use of prompt-chain rather than retrain-chain protocols, and restriction to English text from the C4 corpus.
17	The 15-dimensional hand-crafted feature set yields $\Kempirical$ as an operational lower bound; $\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher.
18	The most marginal configuration (Qwen-cont, $\text{CI}_\text{lower} = 0.6015$) exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.0015, and depth-pair-level feature agreement weakens at the boundary ($\tau \approx 0.37$ at 1v2).
19	
20	Several directions merit future investigation.
21	Validating $\Kempirical$ under retrain-chain protocols---where models are iteratively trained on synthetic data from previous generations---would establish whether the prompt-chain boundary generalizes to the model collapse setting.
22	Scaling to larger models (7B+ and 70B+) would test whether increased generation fidelity shifts $\Kempirical$.
23	Extending to multilingual and multi-domain corpora would assess the generality of the surprisal-based degradation mechanism.
24	Tracking temporal dynamics of web contamination depth in the wild would connect our controlled measurements to real-world corpus evolution.
25	Finally, learned representations may achieve higher $\Kempirical$ than hand-crafted features, narrowing the gap between $\Kempirical$ and $\Ktheory$.
26	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017D89NJyLaFQxgUeEbFsaXe`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\subsection{Theoretical Implications of $\Kempirical = 2$}
5	\label{sec:discuss_kstar}
6	
7	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees the existence of a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
8	Our empirical result---$\Kempirical = 2$ uniformly across all 8 tested configurations (4 model-mode combinations from Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary} and 4 decoding strategies from the ablation)---establishes that this boundary is not only finite but remarkably shallow at the $\leq$1.5B parameter scale.
9	
10	The shallowness of $\Kempirical$ is explained by the rate of information loss across generations.
11	The 0v1 boundary is near-trivially separable ($\auc > 0.99$ across all configurations), reflecting the large distributional gap between human text and first-generation synthetic text.
12	At the 1v2 boundary, $\auc$ drops by approximately 30 percentage points ($\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6955), indicating that the majority of the discriminative signal is consumed in the first generation step.
13	By the 2v3 boundary, $\auc$ falls to 0.5496--0.5893---below the operational threshold $\theta = 0.60$---and deeper pairs converge toward chance.
14	Recursive generation thus acts as a severely lossy channel whose information bottleneck is traversed within two steps.
15	
16	This pattern aligns with findings from the model collapse literature.
17	Shumailov et al.~\cite{shumailov2024collapse} observed that tail-distribution degradation is most acute in the earliest generations, with minority modes vanishing before the output distribution stabilizes.
18	Dohmatob et al.~\cite{dohmatob2024model} derived scaling laws demonstrating that tail probability mass is lost at rates predictable from model and data scale.
19	Our $\Kempirical = 2$ provides a complementary, classifier-based measurement of this phenomenon: the information-theoretic discrimination horizon is reached after just two generation steps.
20	Alemohammad et al.~\cite{alemohammad2024selfconsuming} proved convergence to a fixed point for self-consuming generative models; $\Kempirical = 2$ indicates that, operationally, the approach to this fixed point is rapid enough that adjacent-generation distinctions collapse within two steps.
21	
22	The invariance of $\Kempirical = 2$ across decoding strategies merits emphasis.
23	Greedy decoding, nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$), $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$ all yield $\Kempirical = 2$, with 1v2 $\auc$ spanning a narrow range of [0.6866, 0.7039].
24	Higher decoding entropy modestly increases signal strength ($\auc_{1,2}$: 0.69 greedy $\to$ 0.70 at $T{=}1.2$) but does not shift the boundary.
25	This robustness suggests that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the fundamental information bottleneck of the generation process rather than by surface-level properties of the sampling algorithm.
26	
27	\subsection{Implications of Cross-Model Transfer}
28	\label{sec:discuss_transfer}
29	
30	The cross-model transfer results (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) indicate that depth signals are largely encoded in text-level statistical properties rather than generator-specific artifacts.
31	Classifiers trained on Pythia-1.4B and evaluated on OLMo-1B retain 91--92\% of within-model accuracy, and the broader continuation-mode transfer matrix shows 80--92\% retention across all model pairs.
32	At the 0v1 boundary, cross-model $\auc$ exceeds 0.96 universally, confirming that binary contamination detection is fully transferable across generators.
33	
34	The practical implication is direct: a single depth classifier trained on one model's outputs can be deployed to estimate generational depth for texts produced by other models, without model-specific retraining.
35	This transferability extends the utility of depth estimation to open-web settings where the generating model is unknown---precisely the scenario where depth-aware data curation is most needed.
36	
37	Two caveats bound this conclusion.
38	First, transfer retention of 80--92\% suggests a large text-intrinsic component but does not quantify its exact share; we refrain from decomposing the signal into text-intrinsic and model-specific fractions.
39	Second, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retention (80--85\%) is lower than Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo (91--92\%), indicating that model family similarity modulates transfer even among continuation-mode configurations.
40	
41	\subsection{Feature Importance Across Decoding Strategies}
42	\label{sec:discuss_features}
43	
44	While $\Kempirical = 2$ is invariant to decoding strategy (4/4 configurations), the feature importance ordering is not uniformly consistent.
45	Greedy decoding and $T{=}0.7$ share highly correlated feature rankings (Spearman $\rho = 0.914$), with top features dominated by vocabulary diversity measures: \texttt{self\_bleu}, \texttt{hapax\_ratio}, and \texttt{ttr}.
46	In contrast, greedy versus $T{=}1.2$ yields a substantially lower correlation ($\rho = 0.53$), with $T{=}1.2$ promoting frequency and surprisal features (\texttt{freq\_entropy}, \texttt{hapax\_ratio}, \texttt{surp\_skew}) to the top ranks.
47	
48	This dissociation reveals an important distinction: the \emph{existence} of the depth boundary is robust, but the \emph{optimal feature subset} for detecting it depends on the sampling strategy.
49	High-entropy decoding amplifies frequency-distribution artifacts while attenuating vocabulary-diversity signals, shifting which text properties carry the most discriminative power without altering whether adjacent generations can be distinguished.
50	For practitioners, this implies that a universal depth classifier should incorporate features from multiple groups---surprisal moments, vocabulary diversity, and frequency tail statistics---to maintain performance across unknown decoding conditions.
51	
52	The cross-model feature ordering (Section~\ref{sec:feature_degradation}) provides a complementary perspective: among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly consistent (Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$), with surprisal-variance features---\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}---dominating uniformly.
53	A genuine limitation, however, is that depth-pair-level agreement weakens to $\tau \approx 0.37$ at the 1v2 boundary (Section~\ref{sec:feature_degradation}), indicating that the relative contribution of individual features at the discrimination boundary varies across models even when the aggregate ordering is shared.
54	
55	\subsection{LLM Zero-Shot Baseline}
56	\label{sec:discuss_llm}
57	
58	Frontier LLMs provide a natural baseline for depth estimation.
59	On a 500-sample test set with 5 depth classes (depths 0--4), GPT-5.5 achieves 33.2\% accuracy (MAE = 1.10) and Claude Opus~4.6 achieves 33.1\% accuracy (MAE = 1.15)---both below the OBD-LightGBM baseline of 38.6\% (MAE = 1.11).
60	Simultaneously, both models achieve approximately 88\% binary accuracy on the human-vs.-synthetic distinction (GPT-5.5: 87.8\%, Claude: 88.2\%).
61	
62	This gap between binary detection and depth estimation is informative.
63	Frontier LLMs can reliably determine \emph{whether} a text is machine-generated but cannot estimate \emph{how many generations of recursive processing} it has undergone.
64	Depth estimation requires sensitivity to subtle distributional shifts between adjacent generations---shifts orders of magnitude smaller than the human-vs.-machine gap.
65	The 15-dimensional statistical feature pipeline, despite its simplicity, captures these fine-grained shifts more effectively than frontier LLMs in a zero-shot setting.
66	This motivates the dedicated feature-engineering approach: the task demands specialized statistical sensitivity rather than general language understanding.
67	
68	\subsection{Limitations}
69	\label{sec:limitations}
70	
71	We identify six limitations that bound the generalizability of our findings.
72	
73	\emph{Model scale.}
74	All experiments use models at or below 1.5B parameters (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct).
75	Larger models may produce higher-fidelity text with weaker depth signatures, potentially shifting $\Kempirical$ beyond~2.
76	
77	\emph{Prompt-chain versus retrain-chain.}
78	Our recursive generation protocol is a prompt-chain: the same fixed model repeatedly generates from the previous output.
79	Real-world model collapse involves retrain-chains, where models are retrained on data from earlier generations~\cite{shumailov2024collapse}.
80	Retrain-chains introduce additional degradation through training dynamics, potentially producing different $\Kstar$ values.
81	
82	\emph{Language and domain.}
83	All experiments use English text from the C4 corpus.
84	Depth dynamics may differ across languages, domains (e.g., code, scientific text), and source corpora with different distributional properties.
85	
86	\emph{Decoding coverage.}
87	We test four decoding strategies (greedy, nucleus $p{=}0.9$, $T{=}0.7$, $T{=}1.2$).
88	Other strategies---top-$k$ sampling, beam search, contrastive decoding---remain untested and may yield different feature importance patterns or boundary behavior.
89	
90	\emph{Operational versus theoretical boundary.}
91	$\Kempirical$ is an operational lower bound that depends on the specific feature set and classifier.
92	$\Ktheory$ may be strictly greater; a more expressive classifier or richer feature representation could push $\Kempirical$ higher.
93	
94	\emph{Feature representation.}
95	The 15-dimensional hand-crafted feature set, while interpretable and transferable, is unlikely to be optimal.
96	Learned representations---fine-tuned embeddings or probing classifiers---may capture depth-discriminative patterns that statistical features miss, potentially achieving a higher $\Kempirical$.
97	
98	\paragraph{Graduated Filtering.}
99	% [TODO: exp-006 graduated filtering results pending.
100	% Will discuss: 6 strategies × 5 seeds × α sweep results,
101	% graduated vs binary filtering comparison on MMLU/HellaSwag,
102	% practical implications of coarse 3-class ordinal signal for data curation.]
103	
```

```text
Another worker completed - this one is about reviewing paper sections audit. Let me check what it produced. But first, I was in the middle of reviewing the discussion.tex and conclusion.tex from the paper writing worker. Let me complete that review first.

From my earlier analysis, I found:
1. The greedy vs T=1.2 Spearman ρ = 0.5321 (the paper rounds to 0.53) - this IS in the exp003_analysis.json on the server, just not in the registry. So the number is valid but the registry is incomplete.


2. There's a mismatch in the top 3 features for Config4-T12 - the analysis json lists ["hapax_ratio", "ttr", "surp_skew"] but the discussion section claims ["freq_entropy", "hapax_ratio", "surp_skew"]. I need to look more carefully at what the paper actually says to understand where this discrepancy comes from.

Looking at the single-feature AUC scores in the analysis json, I can see the actual rankings: hapax_ratio scores 0.6775, ttr comes in at 0.6415, surp_skew at 0.6123, and freq_entropy is lower at 0.5905. So the json data shows ttr as the second-ranked feature, not freq_entropy as the paper claims.

The paper's argument about T=1.2 promoting frequency and surprisal features does hold some weight though—freq_entropy jumps from around 0.5139 in greedy to 0.5905 at T=1.2, which is a meaningful improvement even if it doesn't crack the top 3. But there's a clear discrepancy between what the experiment plan lists and what the analysis json actually shows, suggesting the experiment plan data might have come from a different source or calculation method. The paper's phrasing about "promoting" these features to top ranks is technically accurate—freq_entropy does jump significantly from greedy to T=1.2—but it's a bit of a stretch to call them "top 3" when the exact ordering differs depending on which metric I use. Now I need to pull the audit worker's findings and consolidate everything into a comprehensive review.

_source line 128 · 2026-05-13T22:03:02.898Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The analysis artifact confirms:
- "Config1-Greedy vs Config4-T12": ρ = 0.5321, p_value = 0.041159

The paper says ρ = 0.53 which rounds from 0.5321. This is correct. The registry didn't have this value because only 3 of the 6 pairwise Spearman values were stored there (greedy_vs_nuc09, greedy_vs_t07, nuc09_vs_t07).

So the data source exists (exp003_analysis.json on server) but was not propagated to registry. 

The correlation value is accurate, though it should be in the registry for proper provenance tracking. More importantly, that p-value of 0.041 is barely below the 0.05 threshold—with a Bonferroni correction for 6 pairwise comparisons (requiring p < 0.0083), this wouldn't meet a more conservative significance standard. The paper reports ρ = 0.53 without discussing the p-value, which is worth flagging given how marginal the statistical significance actually is. I'm also noticing the top3 features for Config4-T12 in the artifact are ["hapax_ratio", "ttr", "surp_skew"], but I need to check what the experiment plan specified.

Looking at the single-feature AUC values for Config4-T12, hapax_ratio ranks highest at 0.6775, followed by ttr at 0.6415, then surp_skew at 0.6123—so the artifact's ranking matches the AUC scores, but this differs from what the experiment plan and Discussion section list.

The Discussion claims freq_entropy is in the top 3, yet its AUC of 0.5905 places it fifth, well below ttr. This appears to be a factual inconsistency—either the Discussion used a different ranking method like LightGBM feature importance, or there's an error in how the features were selected. The experiment plan might be using LightGBM importance rather than single-feature AUC, or it could have incorrect data. Looking at the Discussion section more carefully, the word "promoting" suggests these features moved up relative to greedy rather than being literally the top-3—freq_entropy jumped from 0.5139 to 0.5905, which is significant but still doesn't make it top-3 by AUC. The Discussion presents these three features as if they're the top performers for T=1.2, which seems misleading given the actual rankings.

The core issue is that the experiment plan lists freq_entropy, hapax_ratio, and surp_skew as top-3 for config4_t12, but the artifact data shows the actual top-3 by single-feature AUC are hapax_ratio, ttr, and surp_skew—freq_entropy replaces ttr in the Discussion's version. This inconsistency suggests either the experiment plan used a different ranking method or there's a factual error that needs clarifying.

Now I'm verifying the specific performance claims: the greedy AUC of 0.6893 and T=1.2 AUC of 0.7039 both check out against what's stated. But there's another discrepancy in the Conclusion—it claims 8 configurations across 3 model families and 2 generation modes, which would suggest 6 model-mode combinations, yet the actual count appears to be only 4 distinct model-mode pairs. So only 4 of the 6 possible model-mode combinations were actually tested—the two rewrite variants for Pythia and OLMo are missing. The Discussion section does correctly specify "4 model-mode combinations," but the Conclusion's phrasing about "3 model families, 2 generation modes" could mislead readers into thinking all 6 were tested. A reviewer might push back on whether the generation mode comparison is valid when it's based only on Qwen data.

Now I'm compiling the full review to send to the Director. Looking at the critical issues: there's a borderline significance problem with the greedy versus T=1.2 comparison (ρ = 0.53, p = 0.041), which wouldn't survive Bonferroni correction across the 6 pairwise comparisons. This value also appears in the server artifact but isn't in the registry. Then there's a discrepancy in Config4-T12 where the top-3 features listed in the Discussion don't match what the experiment actually found. The confidence intervals for both Qwen variants are precariously close to the K*=2 threshold—Qwen-rw's upper bound sits just 0.09 percentage points below 0.60, while Qwen-cont's lower bound is only 0.15 percentage points above it. The paper's writing quality is solid with honest limitations and clear TODOs marked for future work.
```

I also notice the Qwen-rw 2v3 confidence interval upper bound is only 0.09 percentage points below the threshold, which seems marginal but isn't flagged in the paper. The registry appears incomplete too—it's missing the greedy_vs_t12 Spearman correlation that should be there.

Now I need to compile a full review for the director that addresses both workers' findings.

2. **K* boundary yellow band**: Between "1 vs 2" and "2 vs 3", labeled "K* boundary". ✅

3. **θ = 0.60 reference line**: Horizontal dashed line at 0.60, labeled. ✅


4. **Chance line**: At 0.50, labeled. ✅

Now checking the data accuracy across the comparisons: at 0v1 all curves cluster near 1.0 as expected, the 1v2 range sits between 0.61-0.70 above the threshold, and 2v3 drops below 0.60, showing the characteristic sharp decay from the first comparison then gradual decline toward chance. The visual layout is getting crowded with the overlapping lines.

5. **Visual clarity**: The yellow K* band effectively marks the transition zone, and the legend separates the model and decoding groups well enough to read. The crowding at the extremes (near 1.0 and near 0.5) is less critical since the meaningful differentiation happens in the 1v2 region.

6. **Spot-checking key values**: Qwen-rewrite peaks around 0.696 at 1v2, T=1.2 appears to be the highest point near 0.704, OLMo-cont sits around 0.615, and Qwen-cont is tracking lower as expected.

The visualization is solid overall. One subtle issue is that the solid model curves and dashed decoding curves come from different experimental setups—the Qwen-rewrite model line conceptually overlaps with the default decoding parameters, but the figure doesn't make this relationship explicit. Also, the figure currently lives in the artifacts directory rather than the paper figures folder, so it'll need to be moved before the paper can compile, though that's likely a downstream task since it was just generated.

_source line 198 · 2026-05-13T23:28:20.506Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a substantive request to evaluate claim strength. Let me carefully analyze the data and paper_framing.md to provide a thorough assessment. I need to think about this from the perspective of an EMNLP reviewer.

Let me gather the key data points first:

**Current evidence base:**
- K*=2 across 8/8 conditions (4 model-mode + 4 decoding)
- Bootstrap CI: all 1v2 CI_lower > 0.60, all 2v3 CI_upper < 0.60
- Permutation test: all p < 0.002 for 1v2


- Cross-model transfer shows strong retention at 80-92%, with particularly robust transfer between Pythia and OLMo models
- The 0v1 transfer AUC exceeds 0.96 universally, indicating excellent generalization
- Feature ordering stability is strong in continuation mode with Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85, though decoding shows more variability across the 0.53-0.91 range
- LLM zero-shot performance lags behind OBD at 33.2% for GPT and 33.1% for Claude versus 38.6%, but binary classification reaches ~88% accuracy compared to depth-based approaches at ~33%
- Ordinal classification outperforms multiclass on MAE, with 70-85% of the signal appearing to be text-intrinsic

The main constraints are that all tested models stay under 1.5B parameters, the data comes exclusively from English C4, and I'm working with prompt-chain rather than retrain-chain evaluations. The 15 hand-crafted features represent a lower bound on what's possible, and there are some marginal confidence intervals for Qwen that warrant caution.

For whether "suggesting" can be upgraded to something stronger: I have consistent results across 8 conditions which is empirically solid, and DPI provides theoretical grounding, but I'm only looking at 3 model families all under 1.5B, so the evidence supports a careful claim rather than a definitive one.

An EMNLP reviewer would immediately push back on calling anything "universal" with just 3 small models from a single domain. "Suggesting" actually provides appropriate hedging here—upgrading to "establishing" would require broader model coverage, larger scales, and multiple domains. But "suggesting" might undersell the consistency I'm seeing. The better approach is keeping "suggesting" while being more precise about the scope, like specifying that this holds consistently across all tested conditions at the ≤1.5B scale.

The real insight is that my claim's strength is limited by the weakest link—the scale constraint. But within that ≤1.5B boundary, the evidence is actually comprehensive: 8 out of 8 conditions consistent, multiple architectures, different modes and decodings. So I should frame this as demonstrating robustness within the tested scale while suggesting universality.

On the LLM zero-shot finding—GPT-5.5 and Claude both fail at depth estimation while succeeding at binary detection, which does suggest depth estimation is fundamentally different. But it's only two models tested in zero-shot mode, so it's not strong enough as a standalone claim. It works better as a supporting finding that reinforces the main story.

The K* boundary staying invariant across all conditions while feature importance varies significantly is interesting—it suggests the boundary itself is more fundamental than the specific features detecting it. But this feels somewhat expected if K* is truly information-theoretic. It's already in the discussion, so it's better left as a reinforcing point rather than a new independent claim.

Now I'm weighing whether to stick with the current 2+1 structure or expand to 3+1. The main tension is that C1' is already quite substantial with four sub-components, and adding a third claim risks diluting the paper's impact. The options for C2 are all reasonable—distinguishing depth estimation from binary detection, showing feature importance varies while K* doesn't, or highlighting degradation ordering consistency—but none feels essential enough to justify weakening the narrative. C3 on graduated filtering is solid and practical, though it depends on an experiment still running.

The real issue is framing: those four sub-components of C1' need to feel like one unified characterization of K* supported by multiple evidence types, not four separate findings loosely bundled together. That structural clarity matters more than adding another claim.
```

Our analysis reveals generation mode as a significant confound, leading us to recommend continuation mode as the canonical condition for cross-model comparisons.\\\"\\n\\n| Condition | Pythia↔OLMo | Qwen↔others | 论文定位 |\\n|-----------|-------------|-------------|---------|\\n| Continuation | 90-92% retention | 80-85% retention | **主实验条件** — cross-model claims 的基础 |\\n| Rewrite (instruction) | N/A | 61-63% retention | **upper bound with mode-specific confound** — 仅作 ablation |\\n\\n**原则**：\\n- C1' 的跨模型 claim 必须基于 continuation mode 数据\\n- Rewrite mode 的高 AUC (0.6957 vs cont 0.6114) 是 mode-specific signal amplification，不能作为泛化性证据\\n- mvp-001 (Qwen-rewrite) 定位为 \\\"pilot study demonstrating feasibility\\\"，后续用 continuation mode rigorously validate\\n- cont→rw 89% vs rw→cont 82% 不对称性 → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n\\n## Core Claims (2+1 结构, D012)\\n\\n### Primary: C1' (K* Characterization)\\n首次系统测量递归合成文本的 generational discrimination boundary K*，并揭示其 universality。\\n\\nDPI 理论证明有限 K*_theory 必然存在（信息论 impossibility boundary）；我们首次在受控实验中测量 K*_empirical，它提供 K*_theory 的保守下界。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*_empirical 的行为，发现 K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变，展现出跨模型架构、跨解码策略、跨生成模式的 universality。\\n\\n**子组件：**\\n\\n**(a) K*=2 universality across architectures, generation modes, and decoding strategies**\\n> **4/4 model-mode configurations 与 4/4 decoding strategies 全部 K*=2：**\\n>\\n> *Model × mode (exp-007):*\\n> | Model | AUC 1v2 | 95% CI | CI lower > 0.60? |\\n> |-------|---------|--------|-----------------|\\n> | Qwen-rewrite | 0.6955 | [0.6859, 0.7041] | ✅ (+8.6pp) |\\n> | Pythia-1.4B | 0.6539 | [0.6454, 0.6630] | ✅ (+4.5pp) |\\n> | OLMo-1B | 0.6145 | [0.6045, 0.6247] | ✅ (+0.45pp) |\\n> | Qwen-cont | 0.6112 | [0.6015, 0.6213] | ✅ (+0.15pp, 最边缘) |\\n>\\n> *Decoding strategy invariance (exp-003):*\\n> | Decoding | AUC 1v2 | K* |\\n> |----------|---------|-----|\\n> | greedy | 0.6893 | 2 |\\n> | nucleus p=0.9 | 0.6866 | 2 |\\n> | temp=0.7 | 0.6887 | 2 |\\n> | temp=1.2 | 0.7039 | 2 |\\n>\\n> - 全部 1v2 CI lower > 0.60, 全部 2v3 CI upper < 0.60, 全部 permutation p < 0.002\\n> - 4 decoding strategies 的 AUC 1v2 range [0.6866, 0.7039]，K* 完全不变\\n> - K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）\\n> - ⚠️ θ-sensitivity: θ=0.60 下 4/4 K*=2；θ=0.70 下仅 Config4-T12 K*=2（1v2=0.7039 仅超 0.70 阈值 0.004），其余 3 config K*=1。论文以 θ=0.60 为主结论，θ=0.70 边界效应作 footnote\\n> - ⚠️ 特征排序分化: K* 跨 config 稳定，但特征重要性排序不完全一致——Greedy vs T1.2 Spearman=0.53, p=0.041（Bonferroni 校正后阈值 0.0083，不显著）。论文中措辞应为 \\\"suggestive\\\" 而非确定性结论。Config4-T12 正确 top-3 features: hapax_ratio, ttr, surp_skew（非 freq_entropy）\\n> - ⚠️ K*=2 边缘性双端注意: Qwen-cont 1v2 CI lower=0.6015（+0.15pp 高于 θ）+ Qwen-rw 2v3 CI upper=0.5991（-0.09pp 低于 θ），K*=2 边界在两端都极边缘。Discussion/Limitations 需同时提及\\n> - Paper 可写：\\\"K*=2 is invariant across all 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies tested (bootstrap 95% CI for AUC₁ᵥ₂ > 0.60; AUC₂ᵥ₃ CI upper < 0.60), suggesting a universal discrimination boundary for recursive generation at ≤1.5B scale\\\"\\n\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\n> - transfer retention 80-92% suggests depth signal has a large text-intrinsic component（不量化具体比例，因 transfer accuracy retention ≠ 信号分解）\\n> - ⚠️ 数值精度：Pythia→OLMo 91.6%, OLMo→Pythia 90.3%，范围 90-92%（非 91-92%）；Qwen-cont↔Pythia 84.7%/85.2%，范围 84-85%（非 83-85%）\\n\\n**(c) Degradation ordering consistency**\\n> - Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85 on single-feature AUC ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\n\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n1. **\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → 作为 baseline 对比；hand-crafted features 更 interpretable 且支持 degradation ordering 分析\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\n9. **\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\" 升级为 \\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\n\", \"total_chars\": 8603, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

surp_d2_std\\\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\\\n\\\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\\\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\\\\\"），不是独立 claim\\\\n\\\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\\\n\\\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\\\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\\\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\\\\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\\\n\\\\n1. **\\\\\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\\\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\\\\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\\\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\\\n2. **\\\\\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\\\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\\\n3. **\\\\\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\\\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\\\n4. **\\\\\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\\\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\\\n5. **\\\\\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\\\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > ```text
Now I have the full paper_framing.md and all the experimental data. Let me provide my thorough assessment of the three questions.

### Question 1: Can "suggesting" be upgraded?

Current wording: "suggesting a universal discrimination boundary for recursive generation at ≤1.5B scale"

Evidence for upgrading:
- 8/8 conditions, zero exceptions
- Bootstrap CI + permutation test (statistical rigor)
- Cross-model transfer 80-92% (not model-specific artifact)
- DPI theoretical motivation
- Multiple architecture families (GPT-2 style Pythia, OLMo, Qwen)


- Evidence against upgrading:
- Only ≤1.5B parameters (3 models)
- Only English C4
- Only prompt-chain
- K*_empirical is an operational lower bound (depends on features/classifier)
- Qwen-cont marginal: CI_lower = 0.6015, only 0.15pp above θ
- θ = 0.60 is operational choice, not theoretically derived (permutation test justifies but doesn't prove optimality)

My assessment: "suggesting" → "establishing" is too strong given scale limitation. But "suggesting" might be slightly too weak given 8/8 consistency. The right middle ground would be something like "demonstrating" or "identifying" — language that acknowledges the empirical strength while respecting the scope constraints.

Option B feels most defensible: it clearly separates what's definitively shown (K*=2 consistency across all 8 conditions) from what's inferred (universality beyond the tested scale). This distinction makes the claim harder to challenge because I'm not overreaching. For the universal claim specifically, "suggesting" aligns with EMNLP standards when evidence is strong but not conclusive — and the scale limitation is the main constraint here. Within the tested conditions though, I can afford stronger language about the consistency itself.

Now I'm thinking about what other angles could strengthen the narrative beyond just the wording choice.

The zero-shot LLM finding is intriguing — GPT and Claude both hit around 33% on the 5-class task while reaching 88% on binary, which suggests depth estimation is genuinely harder than binary detection. But with only two models, 500 samples, and no exploration of few-shot or fine-tuning approaches, this feels vulnerable to the obvious objection that zero-shot performance on specialized tasks is expected to suffer. It's better positioned as supporting evidence for why specialized features matter rather than standing as its own claim.

The K* boundary consistency is rock solid across all 8 conditions, but feature importance rankings shift considerably (correlations ranging from 0.53 to 0.91). I'm weighing whether this variation in feature importance while K* stays fixed could be its own finding — that the boundary itself is universal even when the mechanisms differ.  The theoretical appeal is there: if K* is truly information-theoretic, it should be more stable than the specific features that detect it, which aligns with the DPI framing. But the statistical picture is messier than I'd like. That ρ=0.53 correlation between greedy and T=1.2 sits right at p=0.041, and once I apply Bonferroni correction across the six pairwise comparisons, it drops below significance. So the "low correlation" claim isn't actually statistically supported. This works well as a discussion point in the existing section, but it's not solid enough to promote to an independent finding.

For the 2+1 structure, I'm weighing whether to add a second contribution claim. The 2+1 setup is clean: C1' characterizes the boundary comprehensively with four sub-components, C3 offers practical utility through graduated filtering, and the narrative flows naturally from understanding to application. That's exactly what EMNLP values. But if I go 3+1, what would C2 even be? The depth estimation comparison is too shallow—just a zero-shot LLM test. The robustness claim is statistically weak. Cross-model transferability is already nested in C1'(b), and pulling it out would dilute the main contribution. The degradation ordering consistency is already C1'(c). Every candidate for C2 either lacks substance or cannibalizes something from C1'.

The real risk is that C1' with four sub-components might read like four separate claims rather than four dimensions supporting one unified claim about K* characterization. I need to frame (a) through (d) as evidence converging on the same insight, not as independent findings. The wording should clarify what's established within the tested scope versus what's suggested more broadly. Beyond that, the structure is solid—2+1 balances depth with practical contribution without overreaching.
```

</details>
